# Consolidated transcription: changes and decisions

Counts refer to meaningful reading loci, including removed or revised doubt marks, and exclude macro-syntax equivalence. A shared locus can count against both witnesses. Retained C doubts do not count as changes. A decision row may also record a rejected reader proposal while retaining both witnesses; such rows contribute zero to the change counts. H description-only pages have no collation rows. The three reader columns preserve competing readings, including when the final adjudication retains a doubt.

## Consequential corrections affecting mathematics

- Scan 011, main paragraph, pi example: The mathematical alphabet is plain P, not H’s blackboard P; the barred double-stroked Q remains a subscript. No unstated projective-space index is added.
- Scan 022, p.116 line -6, original inclusion: The first original subscript resolves to h; the second original capital acquires its own R/K doubt. The written replacement R_h subset Sigma_h is unchanged.
- Scan 022, p.153, final live s-shaped sign: The final s/S-shaped sign has a lower hook and descending stroke omitted by a simple underline. A subscript1 is now an explicit unresolved alternative, with the observed stroke drawn; no formula has been normalized.
- Scan 023, p167 line 10, domain and codomain superscripts: The domain and codomain acquire their own0/o uncertainty marks; the f superscript was already marked. All three raised signs remain unresolved, without changing the clear prime or rightward arrow.
- Scan 024, p224 equality, the second functor occurrence: The second functor occurrence acquires an independent j/J uncertainty; the ink does not establish lowercase despite the same formula context.
- Scan 024, p224 equality, overwritten first argument: The first overwritten functor argument is resolved as live X over cancelled B, from the crossing diagonals rather than the expected formula.
- Scan 024, p224 crowded following note: The question-line functor also acquires its own j/J uncertainty; this does not change or settle the separate B/A numerator.
- Scan 025, p.247, raised mapping index: The raised mapping index is resolved as0 from its own closed oval.
- Scan 025, p247 raised note, subscript of N: The raised N subscript acquires an independent0/i doubt; it is not made certain by the adjacent clear i_0.
- Scan 025, p.247, cancelled lower mapping index: The separately cancelled mapping index acquires an independent0/i doubt; the replacement is not used to restore it.
- Scan 032, p440 cancelled allows one to and its terminal marks: At440 the cancellation certainly covers allows one; to remains live/cancelled uncertain. This changes the scope of the abandoned proposal without changing the later replacement sentence.

- after elementary modelizer: <code>(\unsure{!&#124;;&#124;!,})</code>; alternatives: ! &#124; ; &#124; !,; low. Long upper stroke favors an exclamation mark, but the tailed lower dot does not settle the punctuation.
- canceled mark after Strict test. category: <code>\deleted{\unsure{(!)&#124;!&#124;4.}}</code>; alternatives: (!) &#124; ! &#124; 4.; low. The surviving curve, upright and dot admit more than one underlying reading.
- connector between X and Y: <code>\mathrel{\unsure{-&#124;\to}}</code>; alternatives: dash &#124; right arrow; medium. A downturned thickened endpoint could be a terminal pen stroke or a minimal arrow barb.

### Still unresolved

- Opening, cancelled digit before 41: <code>\deleted{\unsure{3&#124;2}}</code>; alternatives: 3 or 2 under cancellation; low. Dense hatching obscures the original digit, though the deletion extent is certain.
- Opening, between 41 and line: <code>\unsure{-}line</code>; alternatives: hyphen touching l / lead-in belonging to l; medium. The level stroke is physically continuous with l.
- Opening, after avec: <code>\unsure{,&#124;-}</code>; alternatives: comma / short dash; low. The short inclined baseline mark lacks a decisive comma tail or horizontal dash form.
- Opening, before closing parenthesis: <code>\unsure{..&#124;&#x27;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: two dots / closing double quote; low. The two compact marks descend diagonally and their individual shapes remain ambiguous.
- Opening, after closing parenthesis: <code>\unsure{..&#124;.}</code>; alternatives: two dots / one dot plus speck; low. The weaker preceding mark cannot be securely assigned to authored punctuation.
- p66, first occurrence of Latin: <code>\unsure{Latin&#124;latin}</code>; alternatives: Latin / latin; medium. The tall initial with low foot can serve either capital L or lowercase l in this hand.
- p66, goodenough: <code>\unsure{goodenough&#124;good enough}</code>; alternatives: joined / two words; medium. The narrow d-to-e gap favors joining but does not establish intended word spacing.
- p88, around here: <code>\unsure{aroundhere&#124;around here}</code>; alternatives: joined / two words; medium. The d-to-h gap is no larger than some interletter gaps; a compressed word space remains possible.
- p89 line 3, opening quote before to have made me: <code>\unsure{‘&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: single / fused double opening quotation; medium. Only one compact angular mass is visible.
- p89 line -15, opening quote before last not least: <code>\unsure{‘&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: single / fused double opening quotation; medium. The side projection might be part of a single stroke or a second fused stroke.
- p89 line -15, opening quote before last but not least: <code>\unsure{‘&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: single / fused double opening quotation; medium. The opener has one angular mass with a side projection; the double closer is clear.
- p66, second Latin after My: <code>\unsure{Latin&#124;latin}</code>; alternatives: Latin / latin; medium. The tall initial with low foot can serve either capital L or lowercase l in this hand.
- p66, first proposed Latin form: <code>\unsure{Compositum&#124;compositum}</code>; alternatives: capital C; enlarged lowercase c; medium. Initial curve rises above the following o to ascender height, but the sloping line and enlarged initial leave lowercase possible.

Coverage: All three full returns were compared. Eight loci produced different reader readings, and all were inspected on retained crops; both Latin initials were additionally inspected because all reports called them uncertain but the text marked only the first. Final inventory uses B’s 22 rows in physical order, including seventeen entries, three nested underline spans, one deletion and the archive-number comment. Three rejected reader proposals retain C and are shown in the decision table without contributing to change counts. Reader C withdrew its preliminary comma-omission claim before final return because exact C comparison showed those commas already present.

- p.116/p117 boundary quotation pair: <code>\raisebox{-1.1ex}{\unsure{&#x27;&#x27;}}</code>; alternatives: Closing pair after the first inclusion, or opening pair for commutation; one physical pair only; medium. Two strokes are visible low beside n and above-left of commutation. Their quotation shape is clear, but their attribution to one of those two lines is not. Only this one pair is transcribed; no second pair is invented before commutation.
- cancelled p122 reference, leading symbol: <code>\unsure{?}</code>; alternatives: Question-mark-like hook; no secure alternative letter; low. A large upper hook survives, but any lower dot is covered by the crossing strokes. The cancelled extent includes the symbol.
- cancelled p122 reference, star-like symbol: <code>\unsure{*}</code>; alternatives: Asterisk-like mark; exact arms unclear; low. Several intersecting strokes survive between the hook and p. The cancellation prevents separating all of the star arms.
- cancelled p122 reference, trailing symbol: <code>\unsure{?}</code>; alternatives: Question-mark-like form; lower component obscured; low. A tall right hook and an interior upright survive, with their lower part entered by several strikes. Question mark remains a tentative reading.

- p140 overwritten line number: <code>\deleted{\unsure{3}}4</code>; alternatives: Old 3 tentative; live 4 certain; low. The new 4 is clear. An upper loop and middle curved remnant could be an old 3, but the shared strokes do not secure it.

Old and live loops intersect at the re/as boundary. The earlier two-letter reading as and its exact interior contours are not independently recoverable from the crossing; the live whereas is clear.

Coverage: All three finalized full readings, differences, C-context decisions and inventories were compared. Root inspected thirty existing reader crops and three new tight geometry crops. Each witness/reader disagreement has a root crop decision; repeated covered cancellations were considered together only where they occupied the same compact locus. Final81-row inventory follows physical order, with full-page rules first and nested elements next to their text. H has description only. No OCR, outside research or compilation was used; no substantive mathematical check is claimed for this correction-only sheet.

- p156 after corrected line reference3: <code>\unsure{,&#124;.}</code>; alternatives: comma; period; low. The detached low point has a blunt descending edge; its punctuation form remains uncertain.
- p167 line 10, f superscript: <code>f^{\unsure{0&#124;o}}</code>; alternatives: 0 &#124; o; medium. Closed oval does not independently identify numeric zero or lowercase o.
- p167 line 10, first C superscript: <code>C^{\unsure{0&#124;o}}</code>; alternatives: 0 &#124; o; medium. This separate oval remains ambiguous after the formula crop.
- p167 line 10, primed C superscript: <code>{C&#x27;}^{\unsure{0&#124;o}}</code>; alternatives: 0 &#124; o; medium. The prime is clear, but the following round sign is not independently distinguishable.
- p167 line 10, cancelled final sign: <code>\deleted{\unsure{?}}</code>; alternatives: ? is the only attempted textual reading; low. Cancellation obscures the interior; the visible hook is insufficient to resolve it.
- p188 l-9 before whatever: <code>\raisebox{-.45ex}{\deleted{\unsure{a}}}</code>; alternatives: a is tentative; low. Crossed loop remains buried.
- p188 l-9 after whatever: <code>\raisebox{-.45ex}{\deleted{\unsure{s}}}</code>; alternatives: s is tentative; low. A black zigzag cluster does not securely isolate the old letter.
- p188 line 5 before end: <code>\deleted{\unsure{``&#124;`}}</code>; alternatives: double opening pair; single opening stroke; low. The crossed high cluster has joined lobes; their original stroke count remains uncertain.
- 190 l-6 before is it: <code>\unsure{``&#124;`}</code>; alternatives: double opening quotes &#124; single opening quote; low. The fused raised mark lacks a decisive separation.

Coverage: All three full readings, literal changes, doubt/context decisions and inventories compared. Root inspected18 existing reader crops and3 new geometry crops; each reader/witness and reader/reader disagreement was decided on a crop. Re-displays of existing crops are not counted as new crops. All74 inventory elements are represented, including nested states, the reference column, both long rules, bracket and detached stroke, left arrow and isolated point. H has description only, so no H rows. No OCR, outside research or compilation; the isolated mapping is not counted as a proof check.

### Still unresolved

- initial page reference: <code>\unsure{191}</code>; alternatives: 191 remains best; no equally defensible complete numeral alternative; medium. Washed-out numeral strokes remain incomplete after crop 01.
- 193 opening quote deletion state: <code>\unsure{\deleted{``}&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: cancelled double opener; live double opener; low. The separate quote cluster is heavily joined and does not expose a decisive independent crossing.
- p194 cancelled left marginal word: <code>\unsure{stating&#124;starting}</code>; alternatives: stating; starting; low. Beginning clipped and crossed; crop 04.
- Page reference before ls 7 & 8: <code>p\unsure{191&#124;196}</code>; alternatives: 191; 196; medium. The final narrow stroke favours 1 but a faded lower loop for 6 is not excluded.
- 200 opener before it is: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: single bent opener; fused pair; medium. The angular fork is one joined body, not a securely separated pair.
- p206 after l.20: <code>\deleted{\unsure{7&#124;?}}</code>; alternatives: 7; question mark; low. Small crossed upper bar/stem, without a detached question-mark dot or securely closed9 head.
- p210 before I suggest: <code>\unsure{*&#124;\deleted{``}&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: star-like mark; cancelled double opener; joined live double opener; low. Central white opening and crossed projections do not secure identity or cancellation state.
- p216 cancelled entry terminal raised group: <code>\unsure{?&#x27;&#124;&#x27;&#x27;&#124;?&#x27;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: ? followed by one closer; double closer alone; ? followed by a joined double closer; low. ### Still unresolved

- p.234 opener before surely: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: fused double opener; single opener; medium. Joined upper cluster does not separate the stroke count.
- p.234 cancelled high group between ellipses: <code>\deleted{\unsure{&#x27;&#x27;}}</code>; alternatives: tentative cancelled closing quote pair; exact underlying count unresolved; low. The dark overmark conceals the original short strokes.
- p.234 cancelled item before iff: <code>\deleted{\unsure{if}}</code>; alternatives: if is tentative; underlying short item is not securely recoverable; low. Only flattened openings and a low trace survive the crossing.
- p.247 cancelled quoted word: <code>\unsure{aspheric}</code>; alternatives: aspheric tentative; low. Dense diagonal zigzag buries the interior letters.
- p.247 cancelled mapping index: <code>i_{\unsure{0&#124;i}}</code>; alternatives: 0 tentative; low. Low stroke merges with cancellation; clear replacement is not evidence for the old glyph.
- p.247 cancelled terminal quotation: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: closing pair tentative; count unresolved; low. The terminal crossing merges with the high ticks.
- p248 opener before handwritten: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: fused double opener; single opener; medium. Only one connected compact contour survives at quote height.
- 262 cancelled old reference: <code>-\deleted{\unsure{13&#124;l13}}</code>; alternatives: 13; l13; low. Multiple old vertical/loop strokes survive under the crossing; leading minus is certainly live.
- 263 last letter after allow: <code>allow\unsure{e&#124;s}</code>; alternatives: e favoured; s possible; low. The small loop is e-shaped but has a dense retraced lower part.
- p247 raised mapping, N subscript: <code>N_{\unsure{0&#124;i}}</code>; alternatives: 0 &#124; i; low. Open lower cup and detached upper touch remain ambiguous at this separate occurrence.
- p247 cancelled mapping, closing delimiter: <code>\unsure{)}</code>; alternatives: ) conjectured; low. The candidate closing curve cannot be separated securely from the terminal crossing.
- p269 cancelled contraction: <code>\unsure{isn&#x27;t&#124;is&#x27;nt}</code>; alternatives: isn't &#124; is'nt; low. The overstrike prevents recovery of the apostrophe position.
- p269 final exclamation deletion state: <code>\unsure{\deleted{!}&#124;!}</code>; alternatives: included in the cancelled phrase; live uncrossed punctuation; low. Cancellation passes between stem and point and continues beyond both; no direct intersection settles state.

Coverage: All three final full readings, changes, C doubts/context decisions and inventories compared. Root inspected31 distinct existing crops and3 new crops (two long rules and the p267 insertion geometry). Every reader/witness and reader/reader disagreement was decided on a retained crop; re-displays are not new crops. The inventory has84 rows. Thirteen textual/state doubts remain; unassigned purposes of certain geometric marks are separately explained. H has description only and contributes no rows. Four actual limited mathematical check events are retained; the root performed no additional independent mathematical check. No OCR, external research or compilation.

Every reader/witness disagreement was adjudicated on a retained crop. Root reused31 distinct reader crops and made3 narrow extent crops (two black rules and the previously uncropped disputed endpoints of the faint trace); re-displays and one rotation of an existing crop are not new crops. Whole and half images stayed with the three independent readers. The full inventory has64 elements, including35 physical text lines, nested corrections, graphics and archive comment. Sixteen textual/state doubts remain. H is descriptive only; no H comparison rows or mathematical check events are claimed. No OCR, outside research or compilation.

- 329 l.-13, opener before semisimplicial: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: double or single opener; low. Joined apex and two arms do not securely decide one returned stroke versus a joined pair.

### Still unresolved

- p.339 l-12, cancelled sign: <code>\deleted{\unsure{\&amp;&#124;l}}</code>; alternatives: \&; l; low. The crossing hides the lower letter contour; cancellation is certain but neither candidate is excluded.
- p350 l-10, initial underline: <code>\unsure{\uuline{i}&#124;\uline{i}}ndependent</code>; alternatives: \uuline{i}; \uline{i}; medium. Several touching ink passes do not securely distinguish two underline levels from retracing.
- p351 l-11, overwritten old letter: <code>\deleted{\unsure{a&#124;o}}</code>; alternatives: a; o; low. The new a occupies the same space and can supply the apparent stem of an old a.
- Bottom-right cancelled word: <code>\deleted{\unsure{end&#124;ed}}</code>; alternatives: end; ed; low. The cancellation hides the middle of the word; the e-like left loop and final d survive.
- Far-left faint trace, original endpoints: <code>\draw[black!22,line width=.25pt] (58,75)--(57,240)--(55,700)--(53,1100)--(50,1700)--(49,2140);\draw[black!12,line width=.25pt] (49,2140)--(49,2215);</code>; alternatives: A longer trace fading beyond the observed segment; a trace ending within the fading zones; low. The upper crop is entered by the trace, and the lower trace fades into the paper texture. The drawing records only an approximate observed extent, not a sharply established original start/end.
- p341 after better: <code>better\unsure{,&#124;.}</code>; alternatives: comma; period; medium. Low oblique filled body with no independently resolved tail.
- p341 l7 opener: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;``}doesn&#x27;t&#x27;&#x27;</code>; alternatives: single; fused double; medium. Looped high body and projecting tips leave count unresolved.
- p.345 closing parenthesis: <code>(again\tikz[baseline=0pt,x=1ex,y=1ex]{\draw[line width=.08ex,line cap=round] (.10,1.6) .. controls (.53,.8) and (.52,.05) .. (-.12,-.48);\draw[line width=.08ex,line cap=round] (.46,.04)--(.92,.04);}</code>; alternatives: attached punctuation; nonpunctuating transverse extension; medium. Connected curve and short bar are reproduced; their function is uncertain.
- p350 after also: <code>also \unsure{in&#124;on} l.15.</code>; alternatives: in; on; medium. Closed low first body and high point support competing readings.
- p353 l.9 opener: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}its&#x27;&#x27;</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. Fused tips and narrow slot do not decide original stroke count.

Coverage: All three final full readings, reports and inventories were compared. All four explicit C doubts and five context issues have decisions. Root adjudicated all reader/witness differences on retained crops, including the rejected candidate reversal, material-circle drawings and continuity proposals. Root reused24 reader crops and generated18 new crops (two narrow complete rules and16 isolated-mark loci, including three close two-mark groups); all were actually displayed. Failed/truncated displays were retried from existing files, without recropping. The inventory has65 elements and10 remaining physical doubt loci. Isolated dark marks are retained with unassigned origin; photographic texture and material features are distinguished in the notes. H is description-only; no OCR, outside research, compilation or mathematical proof was performed.

- p351 l-11, cancelled underlying letter: <code>\deleted{\unsure{a&#124;o}}</code>; alternatives: \deleted{\unsure{a&#124;o}}; low. The older oval lies beneath the live a in one letter space. Its surviving contour does not decide a versus o; overlap placement, rather than the interior letter, is resolved.
- p356, cancelled Formulae/Formulæ: <code>\unsure{Formulae&#124;Formulæ}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{Formulae&#124;Formulæ}; medium. The final ae-like bodies lie beneath a long crossing stroke. Their join could be ordinary connected a/e or a ligature; the cancellation prevents a secure separation.
- p357, cancelled argument body: <code>\unsure{\beta&#124;g&#124;S}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{\beta&#124;g&#124;S}; low. Two loop-like bodies and a descending continuation favour beta. The broad diagonal cancellation supplies or obscures critical joins, leaving g and S possible.
- p357, cancelled argument subscript: <code>\unsure{M&#124;m}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{M&#124;m}; low. Low short humps remain below the argument body. The covered left edge and crowded baseline do not decide upper versus lower case.
- p357, cancelled relation sign: <code>\unsure{=&#124;-}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{=&#124;-}; low. There are two short roughly parallel horizontal/oblique remnants, but one may be part of the cancellation. Equality and a single dash both remain.
- p357, cancelled terminal sign: <code>\unsure{?&#124;s}</code>; alternatives: ?; s; low. A separate low point favors question mark; the overwritten upper curve remains ambiguous.
- p360, word before looking: <code>\unsure{than&#124;then}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{than&#124;then}; medium. The small loop after th is closed but its exit is merged into the following n. An a is favoured; a rapid e remains permitted by the ink.
- bottom-right cancelled marginal cluster: <code>\deleted{\unsure{end&#124;ed}}</code>; alternatives: \deleted{\unsure{end&#124;ed}}; low. The cancellation runs across a pale e-like beginning, indistinct middle strokes and a tall final d-like stem. The middle n is not secure. The low traces meet the sheet edge; no further letters are supplied.
- C_context: p353 opening quotation count: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{``&#124;'}; medium. The joined oblong/fork is visible, but it does not securely decide one versus two original strokes.
- p341, after better: <code>better\unsure{,&#124;.}</code>; alternatives: better\unsure{,&#124;.}; medium. The mark is a filled oblique stroke tapering down-left. A comma is favoured; the short thick body does not exclude an oblique full stop.
- p341 l7, opening quotation before doesn't: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{'&#124;``}; medium. The opener has one dominant hooked stroke and a small joined upper spur. A single mark is favoured but a fused double remains possible.
- p350, after also: <code>\unsure{in&#124;on}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{in&#124;on}; medium. The first small body is looped, with a detached high point nearby. The point favours in, but the closed low body also allows on.
- p358 l.9, opening quotation before been: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{``&#124;'}; medium. The opener is an arch with two short downward legs joined above. A fused double is favoured, but a single returned mark remains possible.
- p358 continuation l-4, opening quotation before commutes: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{``&#124;'}; medium. One dark upright is followed by a detached paler high point at quotation height. It may be the weakened second quote, but its shape is too short to decide the count.
- p361 l16, opening quotation before didn't: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{'&#124;``}; medium. One dominant hooked stroke has a joined right-hand prong. The joined shape does not establish whether it was one quote or a fused pair.
- p365 l-19, opening quotation before first isn't: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{``&#124;'}; medium. The opening group is a short joined inverted-U/arch. It supports a joined double but does not securely exclude one returned quotation stroke.
- p369 l.4, opening quotation before suggests: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: \unsure{'&#124;``}; medium. The opener is a tall bent stroke with a shorter joined right prong. One quote is favoured, but the fused pair alternative remains possible.
- p350 l-10, opener before independent: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. Joined upright mass and small side branch.
- p355 l-3, opener before from: <code>\unsure{``&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. Two legs joined at one apex; stroke count uncertain.
- p362 l.7, opener before interpret: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: single; double; medium. Tall arm and shorter connected branch.

Coverage: Three final full readings, literal changes, explicit C doubts, context decisions, notes and complete inventories were compared. Root viewed all33 A crops plus C01/C19/C23 and B12,37 distinct existing crops in total; no new crop was necessary. All reader/witness and reader/reader differences were adjudicated, including rejected ae resolution, candidate-order differences, material/ink treatment and quotation counts. All14 explicit C uncertainty macros have recorded decisions; four are resolved, one is reordered, the others retained. The shared upper text was judged independently from scan028. No recognition, outside research or compilation was used.

### Still unresolved

- p371 reference separator: <code>l\unsure{-&#124;.}14</code>; alternatives: dash; elongated stop; low. Two touching lobes are short and horizontal.
- p372 final word: <code>\unsure{unverse&#124;inverse&#124;universe}</code>; alternatives: unverse; inverse; universe; low. Initial joined minims lack a decisive independent dot.
- p373 recommendation opener: <code>\unsure{``&#124;`}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. Two limbs share one apex.
- p380 closing mark: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#124;&#x27;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: one visible closer; possible fused second closer; medium. One detached slant survives to right of ff descenders; no distinct second partner.
- p381 separator: <code>\unsure{-&#124;.}</code>; alternatives: dash; elongated stop; medium. Low short bent horizontal group.
- p381 old digit: <code>\deleted{\unsure{8}}</code>; alternatives: 8 favored; hidden old contour; low. Live3 obscures the older loops.
- p383 bothering closer: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#x27;&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. A thick joined patch has unequal pendant branches.
- p383 suggest opener: <code>\unsure{``&#124;`}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. One unbroken arch with two low ends.
- Formula7 preceding mark: <code>\unsure{\raisebox{1.1ex}[0pt][0pt]{$+$}&#124;``&#124;`}</code>; alternatives: raised plus-shaped cross; double quotation; single quotation; low. Transverse strokes decide visible cross shape, but not the underlying sign identity.
- p387 cancelled ending: <code>\deleted{\unsure{s}}</code>; alternatives: s favored; interior hidden; low. Oblique cancellation covers the lower curve.
- p388 terminal mark: <code>\unsure{,&#124;.}</code>; alternatives: comma; period; medium. Short descending oblique with curved foot.
- p395 interpreted opener: <code>\unsure{``&#124;`}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. Unequal limbs meet at top.
- p395 saying closer: <code>\unsure{&#x27;&#x27;&#124;&#x27;}</code>; alternatives: double; single; medium. Two lower tips share one angled upper mass.
- page reference after396: <code>p.39\unsure{7&#124;1}</code>; alternatives: 397;391; medium. Short top hook and descending stem do not exclude1.
- p401 formula opener: <code>\unsure{`&#124;``}</code>; alternatives: single; double; medium. One continuous body with short interior prong.
- archive corner, comment only: <code>23 (possibly29)</code>; alternatives: 23;29; medium. Open upper turn favors3 but blurred closure permits9.

Coverage: All three final readings, changes, six explicit C uncertainty loci, archive uncertainty, contextual decisions, notes and inventories compared. Root viewed39 existing reader crops and one new crop covering the previously uncovered p380 closer and connecting ff descenders; all actual disputes were adjudicated. Every separate correction entry and nested mark is inventoried in physical order. C_context extraction was corrected equally for all readers without reopening page/half images. No OCR, outside research or compilation was used.
